tbmm-2022-03-23sit04spe0241par0156	Mr. President, dear MPs, I salute the Veteran Assembly. The concept of protecting the consumer, although modern - day, after the 1950s, becomes important in the world, is based on a much older understanding of our culture and traditions. In the 13th century, the Abyssinian institution, which emerged in the Anatolian Seljucians, worked as a trade organisation operating in the protection of consumers. The deception of the consumer is also forbidden according to our great religion, and according to Islam, the most important principle of trade morality is integrity and honesty. The prophet also stressed the importance of the matter by saying that he who deceives us is not one of us. The concept of protecting the consumer is a dynamic concept that is constantly developing in order to adapt to the changes in the shopping culture, in line with the change of social life. Until 1995, the protection of consumers was tried to be organised by general law, and the first time in 1995, the Law on the Protection of consumer number 4077 was set up as a special law. As I mentioned earlier, the protection of consumers is a changing and dynamic area of law. Thus, in 2013, the Law on the Protection of consumer number 4077 was lifted and Law on the Protection of consumer number 6502 was enforced in order to comply with the changing market requirements. However, during the period from 2013 to today, there have been significant changes in commercial life, both globally and commercial life, and there have been significant improvements and changes in shopping culture, sales channels, especially in the pandemic process of the world. The need for changes has also been made in Law 6502, which is available to protect our consumers and the rights and obligations of all parties who offer goods and services to consumers at a higher level in these changing conditions. The law proposed in this framework implements regulations to protect consumers' economic interests. If the consumer defaults, the new arrangement will be made in favour of the consumer's demand for the entire debt. Developments in IT systems have led to a huge increase in the volume of e-trade worldwide, including our country, with the ease of access to technology, the speed of processing and cost of digitalisation, and the effects of the coronavirus epidemic. This has led to many and diverse numbers of vendors and products, institutional structures, the continuity of campaigns and discounts, and the fact that most of the distant contracts where consumers are on the side have been involved in e-trade platforms that operate as intermediary providers. With this change in the E-trade sector taken into account, responsibilities and obligations are brought to the middleman's service providers regarding distant contracts where consumers are on the side. With changes to the time-off agreements, which lead to the consumer's victims, consumer problems in this sector will be reduced to a minimum. In an effort to encourage sustainable consumption and encourage savings to prevent waste, renewed products are under law regulation for the first time, and new rights for post-sale services are being granted. The 21st century moved digital and green transformation to the center of the global agenda. In particular, a sense of savings-oriented consumption must be established for sustainable and efficient resource management in the face of rapid change and transformation in electronic products.

Although the legal system for the protection of the consumer has emerged from the second half of the 20th century, the Athletic organization, which is an important place in the Turkish state tradition, has organised producer-gatherer interests starting in the mid-13th century and does not disagree with each other in any way, maintaining production and consumer activities at that time, with the harmonised composition of morality and art. As is the case at the time, it was intended for all people to live in peace by establishing good relations and balance between the nation and the state and all institutions of society. Thus, as a continuation of this long-standing tradition, some laws in Turkey that protected consumers, even indirectly in the Republic era, took place in Article 172 (182) of the Constitution of 1982, where the state's protection of consumers is a constitutional duty. Over the course of the next period, the law on the protection of consumer number 4077, which has been inadequate to protect consumers and has been established by a special law that protects consumers, which has changed and developed and developed sales techniques and is institutionalised by companies. The 4077 Law adopted a comprehensive change in 2003, providing new rights to consumers, and in 2014 the law was lifted from the law, enlarging consumer protection for consumers, and the Law on the Protection of consumers, 6502. However, due to a very dynamic area of consumer protection in the coming process, a very rapid change and development in trade life in our country and in the world, the current Law of 6502 needed to change. Article 10 of the proposed proposal is obliged to record information about all authorized service stations by the Ministry of Commerce for producers and importers, so that consumers, companies, can access the correct and current service station information, and not be victimized by any deceptive application. In the 12th article, consumer arbitration committees are easily accessible as non-judgmental decisionmakers, in order to ensure that consumer discrepancies are fair, quick, simple, and at the least possible cost. From 1995, when the consumer arbitration committees were formed, they decided on about 16 million applications today, reducing the workload on our country's judiciary significantly. In 2021, consumer arbitration committees received 537,479 applications to resolve conflicts between consumers and sellers or providers, and the economic value of applications reached $870m. Under the arrangement, the protection of consumers as a more professional centre of referee delegations has been organised and the jurisdiction of the market surveillance administration has been determined. The 13th article has also been reorganised by the arbitration committees to make a final decision. As of 2022, the monetary value has been raised to 30,000 Turkish liras in conflictes under $15,430, in order to ensure that more discrepancies can be addressed with a rapid, simple, and minimum expense settlement while the consumer referees are on duty. The consumer arbitration committee is crucial to the accessibility of consumer arbitration committees after resolving its jurisdictions. The amendment to provide this allowed the consumer to apply for a subsidiary of the place without a consumer arbitration committee in the area where the consumer's settlement or consumer process is being processed, without any loss of rights. The 14th article has enabled consumer arbitration committees to submit electronically to the parties or representatives of the conflict of information and request of documents and other correspondence and decisions seen. In this scope, both the failures during postal notifications and shortening the expected deadlines and reducing the mail costs of consumer referees. About 10 million Turkish liras have been saved in this area in a year. The 17th article, however, has brought a number of arrangements for the half-time holidays. The goal was to address the losses caused by blocking the sale of the circuit holidays, which were sold without end of the dwelling, which was paid in advance. It is also within the scope of the law to sell time vacations through financial rentals. This period, which is possible to be rented for up to 90 years, has been limited to a ten-year period of vacations, allowing problems related to various issues to be resolved.
